L2141: ## 1. Summary
L2142: In this submission the sponsor included reports of two animal carcinogenicity studies, one in Sprague-dawley rats and one in CB6F1-Tg rasH2 mice. These studies were intended to assess the carcinogenic potential of E2006 when administered orally by gavage at appropriate drug levels for 104 weeks in rats and 26 weeks in mice.
L2143: **Rat Study:** Three hundred Sprague Dawley rats of each sex were randomly assigned to the three treated and two vehicle control groups in equal size of 60 rats per group. The dose levels for treated groups in male rats were 30, 100, and 300 mg/kg/day. The dose levels for treated groups in female rats were 10, 30 and 100 mg/kg/day. The rats in the vehicle control groups received the vehicle(0.5 w/v% methylcellulose solution/1 mol/L hydrochloric acid [4:1, v/v]). The study for the rats was designed to continue for up to 104 weeks; and all surviving male rats were sacrificed during Week 105.
L2144: The survival analyses did not show a statistically significant dose response relationship in mortality across the combined vehicle control group and treated groups for either males or females. The pairwise comparisons did not show statistically significant differences in mortality between the combined vehicle control group and each of the treated groups for either males or females, except the differences between the combined vehicle control group and the 10 mg/kg/day group in females. However, the difference between the combined vehicle control group and the 10mg/kg/day group is a negative finding.
L2145: The tumor analysis did not show any tumor types with a statistically significant positive dose response in either males or females. The pairwise comparisons did not show statistically significant increases in incidence in any observed tumor types in any treated groups in either males or females.
L2146: **Mouse Study:** One hundred CB6F1-Tg rasH2 mice of each sex were randomly assigned to the three treated and vehicle control group in equal size of 25 mice per group. There were 15 mice of each sex in the positive control group. The dose levels for treated groups were 50, 150, and 500 mg/kg/day. The mice in the vehicle control group received the vehicle (0.5 w/v% methylcellulose solution/1 mol/L hydrochloric acid (4:1, v/v)). The study was designed to continue for up to 26 weeks for both sexes and all surviving mice were sacrificed during Week 27. The mice in the positive control group received a single intraperitoneal injection of N-nitroso-N-methylurea (MNU) at 75 mg/kg on Day 1. Positive control animals were sacrificed on Day 134 (males) or Day 154 (females).
L2147: The survival analyses did not show a statistically significant dose response relationship in mortality across vehicle control and treated groups for either males or females. The pairwise comparisons did not show any statistically significant differences in mortality between the vehicle control and each of the treated groups for either males or females.
L2148: The pairwise comparisons between the vehicle control group and the positive control group showed a statistically significant increase in mortality in the positive control group for both the male and female mice.
L2149: The trend test showed a statistically significant positive dose response relationship in incidence of hemangiosarcoma in spleen (p-value=0.0129&lt;0.05) and the combined tumors of hemangiosarcoma and hemangioma in the whole body (p-value=0.0205&lt;0.05) between the vehicle control and the treated groups in male mice
L2150: The pairwise comparisons between the vehicle control and the positive control showed statistically significant increases in incidence of malignant lymphoma in hematopoietic (p-value&lt;0.001), forestomach papilloma in stomach (p-value&lt;0.001) and squamous cell carcinoma in stomach (p-value=0.0017&lt;0.05) in male mice; malignant lymphoma in hematopoietic (p-value&lt;0.001), papilloma in skin (p-value&lt;0.001) and forestomach
L2151: papilloma in stomach (p-value&lt;0.001) in female mice.
L2152: ## 2. Background
L2153: In this submission the sponsor included reports of two animal carcinogenicity studies, one in Sprague-dawley rats and one in CB6F1-Tg rasH2 mice. These studies were intended to assess the carcinogenic potential of E2006 when administered orally by gavage at appropriate drug levels for 104 weeks in rats and 26 weeks in mice. Results of this review have been discussed with the reviewing pharmacologist Dr. Avila. This review analyzed the SAS data sets of these studies received from the sponsor on March 1, 2017 via IND111871/Sequence 0105.
L2154: In this review the phrase "dose response relationship" refers to the linear component of the effect of treatment, and not necessarily to a strictly increasing or decreasing mortality or tumor incidence rate as the dose increases.
L2155: ## 3. Rat Study
L2156: Two separate experiments were conducted, one in males and one in females. In each of these two experiments there were three treated groups and two vehicle control groups. Three hundred Sprague Dawley rats of each sex were randomly assigned to the three treated and the two vehicle control groups in equal size of 60 rats per group. The dose levels for treated groups in male rats were 30, 100, and 300 mg/kg/day. The dose levels for treated groups in female rats were 10, 30 and 100 mg/kg/day. The rats in the vehicle control group received the vehicle(0.5 w/v% methylcellulose solution/1 mol/L hydrochloric acid [4:1, v/v]). The study for the rats was designed to continue for up to 104 weeks, and all surviving male rats were sacrificed during Week 105.

L2159: ## 3.1. Sponsor's analyses
L2160: ## 3.1.1. Survival analysis
L2161: For survival rate analysis, the survival curves (Kaplan-Meier's curves) for individual groups were estimated by Kaplan-Meier's method (product limit estimator) (Kaplan and Meier, 1958). Survival rates in the Control-1 and Control-2 groups were analyzed by log-rank test. Then, when there were no significant differences, the combined control group (combination of the Control-1 and Control-2 groups) was treated as the substantive control group and the following analysis was conducted. When there were some significant differences, the Control-1, Control-2 and combined control groups were treated as the independent control groups and the following analysis was conducted. The decreased trend of survival rate to dose level was analyzed by Tarone-type method, and then the differences between the control group and each dose group were compared using log-rank test. Tarone-type method and log-rank test were conducted at a significance level of 5% in upper probability level.
L2162: Sponsor's findings: Sponsor's analysis showed the numbers (percents) of death were 15 (25%), 18 (30%), 13 (47%), 20 (33.3%) and 17 (28.3%) in vehicle control 1, vehicle control 2, 30 mg/kg/day, 100 mg/kg/day and 300 mg/kg/day dose groups, respectively in males and 15 (25%), 17 (28.3%), 7 (11.7%), 9 (15%) and 9
L2163: (15%) in vehicle control 1, vehicle control 2, 10 mg/kg/day, 30 mg/kg/day and 100 mg/kg/day dose groups, respectively in females.
L2164: The sponsor made the following conclusions: there was no significantly decreased trend on the survival rate related to dose level in males or females.
L2165: ## 3.1.2. Tumor data analysis
L2166: For tumor incidence analysis, Fisher's exact test was applied for all tumors to assess the differences of tumor incidence between the Control-1 and Control-2 groups. Then, when there were no significant differences, the combined control group (combination of the Control-1 and Control-2 groups) was treated as the substantive control group and the following analysis was conducted. When there were some significant differences, the Control-1, the Control-2 and the combined control groups were treated as the independent control groups and the following analysis was conducted. The above test was conducted at a significance level of 5% in two-tailed level. High incidence tumors (10 animals or more animals per sex) were analyzed by the survival-adjusted Peto's mortality-prevalence method to assess an increasing trend of incidence to dose level for all groups (positive trend), and to compare the incidence between the each control group and each dose group (pairwise comparison). Low incidence tumors (less than 10 animals per sex) were analyzed by Peto's exact permutation test to assess for analyzing positive trend and pairwise comparison. In the incidental tumor analysis, the fixed intervals were defined as follows: Weeks 1-50, 51-80, 81-104 and scheduled terminal sacrifice. Significance level for analyzing positive trend was 1% in one-tailed level for common tumors and 5% in one-tailed level for rare tumors, and for pairwise comparison was 5% in one-tailed level for both common and rare tumors. Common tumors were defined as those exceeding 1% (&gt;1\%) and rare tumors as 1% or less (\le 1\%) in the historical background data at (b) (4) (Mary and Charles, 2004).
L2167: Sponsor's findings: The sponsor's analyses did not show a statistically significant dose response relationship among the treated and vehicle groups in any of the observed tumor types in male or female rats.
L2168: ## 3.2. Reviewer's analyses
L2169: To verify sponsor's analyses and to perform additional analysis suggested by the reviewing pharmacologist, this reviewer independently performed survival and tumor data analyses. Data used in this reviewer's analyses were provided by the sponsor electronically on March 1, 2017 via IND111871/Sequence 0105. Note that, in the submitted tumor data, the two identical vehicle control groups were combined. Therefore, all statistical comparisons were between combined vehicle control group and treated groups."
L2170: ## 3.2.1. Survival analysis
L2171: The survival distributions of animals in all four groups were estimated by the Kaplan-Meier product limit method. The dose response relationship and homogeneity of survival distributions were tested for combined vehicle control, low, medium, and high dose groups using the Likelihood Ratio test and the Log-Rank test. The intercurrent mortality data are given in Tables 7 and 8 in the appendix for males and females, respectively. The Kaplan-Meier curves for survival rate are given in Figures 1 and 2 in the appendix for males and females, respectively. Results of the tests for dose response relationship and homogeneity of survivals, are given in Tables 9 and 10 in the appendix for males and females, respectively.
L2172: Reviewer's findings: This reviewer's analysis showed the numbers (percents) of death were 33 (27.5%), 13 (21.67%), 20 (33.3%), and 17 (28.3%) in male rats for the combined vehicle control group, 30 mg/kg/day, 100
L2173: mg/kg/day and 300 mg/kg/day groups respectively; and 32 (26.7%), 7 (11.67%), 9 (15%), and 9 (15%) in female rats for the combined vehicle control group, 10 mg/kg/day, 30 mg/kg/day and 100 mg/kg/day groups, respectively.
L2174: The survival analyses did not show a statistically significant positive dose response relationship in mortality across combined vehicle control group and treated groups for either males or females. The pairwise comparisons did not show any statistically significant differences in mortality between the combined vehicle control group and each of the treated groups for either males or females except the differences between the combined vehicle control group and the 10 mg/kg/day group in females. However, the difference between the combined vehicle control group and the 10 mg/kg/day group is a negative finding.
L2175: ## 3.2.2. Tumor data analysis
L2176: The tumor data were analyzed for the positive dose response relationships and the positive pairwise comparison increases between each of the treated groups with control group. Both the dose response relationship tests and pairwise comparisons were performed using the Poly-K method described in the paper of Bailar and Portier (1988) and Bieler and Williams (1993). In this method an animal that lives the full study period (w_{max}) or dies before the terminal sacrifice but develops the tumor type being tested gets a score of s_h = 1. An animal that dies
L2177: at week w_h without a tumor before the end of the study gets a score of s_h = \left(\frac{w_h}{w_{max}}\right)^k &lt; 1. The adjusted group size is defined as \sum s_h. As an interpretation, an animal with score s_h = 1 can be considered as a whole animal while an animal with score s_h &lt; 1 can be considered as a partial animal. The adjusted group size \sum s_h is equal to N (the original group size) if all animals live up to the end of the study or if each animal that dies before the terminal sacrifice develops at least one tumor, otherwise the adjusted group size is less than N. These adjusted group sizes are then used for the dose response relationship (or the pairwise) tests using the Cochran-Armitage test. One critical point for Poly-k test is the choice of the appropriate value of k, which depends on the tumor incidence pattern with the increased dose. For long term 104 week standard rat and mouse studies, a value of k=3 is suggested in the literature. Hence, this reviewer used k=3 for the analysis of this data. For the calculation of p-values the exact permutation method was used. The tumor rates and the p-values for the positive dose response relationship tests and pairwise comparisons are listed in Tables 11 and 12 in the appendix for male and female rats, respectively.
L2178: Adjustment for multiple testing: For the adjustment of multiple testing of dose response relationship for a submission with one chronic rat study and one transgenic mouse study, the more recently revised draft (January, 2013) FDA guidance for the carcinogenicity studies suggests the use of test levels \alpha = 0.005 for common tumors and \alpha = 0.025 for rare tumors for the chronic rat study. For pairwise comparisons for the chronic rat study in the above type of submission with one chronic rat study and one transgenic mouse study, the same guidance document suggests the use of test levels \alpha = 0.01 for common tumors and \alpha = 0.05 for rare tumors for the chronic rat study.
L2179: It should be noted that the FDA guidance for multiple testing for dose response relationship is based on a publication by Lin and Rahman (1998). In this work the authors investigated the use of this rule for Peto analysis. However, in a later work Rahman and Lin (2008) showed that this rule for multiple testing for dose response relationship is also suitable for Poly-K tests.
L2180: Reviewer's findings: Following tumor types showed p-values less than or equal to 0.05 either in tests for dose response relationship or in pairwise comparisons between the vehicle control group and each of the treated groups.

L2184: ## 4. Mouse Study
L2185: Two separate experiments were conducted, one in males and one in females. In each of these two experiments there were three treated groups, one vehicle control group, and one positive control group. One hundred CB6F1-Tg rasH2 mice of each sex were randomly assigned to the treated and vehicle control group in equal size of 25 mice per group. There were 15 mice of each sex in the positive control group. The dose levels for treated groups were 50, 150, and 500 mg/kg/day. The mice in the vehicle control group received the vehicle (0.5 w/v% methylcellulose solution/1 mol/L hydrochloric acid (4:1, v/v)). The study was designed to continue for up to 26 weeks for both sexes and all surviving mice were sacrificed during Week 27. The mice in the positive control group received a single intraperitoneal injection of N-nitroso-N-methylurea (MNU) at 75 mg/kg on Day 1. Positive control animals were sacrificed on Day 134 (males) or Day 154 (females).

L2217: ## 5. Conclusion
L2218: In this submission the sponsor included reports of two animal carcinogenicity studies, one in Sprague-dawley rats and one in CB6F1-Tg rasH2 mice. These studies were intended to assess the carcinogenic potential of E2006 when administered orally by gavage at appropriate drug levels for 104 weeks in rats and 26 weeks in mice.
L2219: **Rat Study:** Three hundred Sprague Dawley rats of each sex were randomly assigned to the three treated and the two vehicle control groups in equal size of 60 rats per group. The dose levels for treated groups in male rats were 30, 100, and 300 mg/kg/day. The dose levels for treated groups in female rats were 10, 30 and 100 mg/kg/day. The rats in the vehicle control groups received the vehicle(0.5 w/v% methylcellulose solution/1 mol/L hydrochloric acid [4:1, v/v]). The study for the rats was designed to continue for up to 104 weeks and all surviving male rats were sacrificed during Week 105.
L2220: The survival analyses did not show a statistically significant dose response relationship in mortality across the combined vehicle control group and treated groups for either males or females. The pairwise comparisons did not show statistically significant differences in mortality between the combined vehicle control group and each of the treated groups for either males or females, except the differences between the combined vehicle control group and the 10 mg/kg/day group in females. However, the difference between the combined vehicle control group and the 10mg/kg/day group is a negative finding.
L2221: The tumor analysis did not show any tumor types had a statistically significant positive dose response in either males or females. The pairwise comparisons did not show any statistically significant increases in incidence in any observed tumor types in any treated groups in either males or females.
L2222: **Mouse Study:** One hundred CB6F1-Tg rasH2 mice of each sex were randomly assigned to the treated and vehicle control group in equal size of 25 mice per group. There are 15 mice of each sex in the positive control group. The dose levels for treated groups were 50, 150, and 500 mg/kg/day. The mice in the vehicle control group received the vehicle (0.5 w/v% methylcellulose solution/1 mol/L hydrochloric acid (4:1, v/v)). The study was designed to continue for up to 26 weeks for both sexes and all surviving mice were sacrificed during Week 27. The mice in the positive control group received a single intraperitoneal injection of N-nitroso-N-methylurea (MNU) at 75 mg/kg on Day 1. Positive control animals were sacrificed on Day 134 (males) or Day 154 (females).
L2223: The survival analyses did not show a statistically significant dose response relationship in mortality across vehicle control and treated groups for either males or females. The pairwise comparisons did not show any statistically significant differences in mortality between the vehicle control and treated groups for either males or females.
L2224: The pairwise comparisons showed a statistically significant increase in mortality in the positive control group for both the male and female mice.
L2225: The trend test showed statistically significant positive dose response relationship in incidence of hemangiosarcoma in spleen (p-value=0.0129&lt;0.05) and combined tumors of hemangiosarcoma and hemangioma in the whole body (p-value=0.0205&lt;0.05) between the vehicle control and the treated groups in male mice
L2226: The pairwise comparisons between the vehicle control and the positive control showed statistically significant increases in incidence of malignant lymphoma in hematopoietic (p-value&lt;0.001), forestomach papilloma in stomach (p-value&lt;0.001) and squamous cell carcinoma in stomach (p-value=0.0017&lt;0.05) in male mice; malignant lymphoma in hematopoietic (p-value&lt;0.001), papilloma in skin (p-value&lt;0.001) and forestomach papilloma in stomach (p-value&lt;0.001) in female mice.

L2285: Figure 1 displays the effects of Lemborexant (1 mg/kg, 10 mg/kg, 30 mg/kg) on sleep/wake architecture and cataplexy in Wild-Type (WT) and Orexin Knock-Out (KO) mice, comparing vehicle (dashed line) and drug (solid line) administration. The time of vehicle/drug jelly (18:45) and chocolate (19:00) presentation is indicated by arrows.
A: Hourly amounts of wake, NREM sleep, REM sleep, and cataplexy (%/hr) over 6 hours (16:00 to 22:00).
WT Mice: Lemborexant significantly increases wake time (10 mg/kg, 30 mg/kg) and NREM sleep time (10 mg/kg, 30 mg/kg) compared to vehicle, particularly during the 19:00-22:00 period. REM sleep time is significantly decreased (10 mg/kg, 30 mg/kg). Cataplexy time is significantly increased (10 mg/kg, 30 mg/kg).
Orexin KO Mice: Lemborexant does not significantly alter wake time, NREM sleep time, REM sleep time, or cataplexy time compared to vehicle.
B: Average amount of wake/NREM sleep/REM sleep/cataplexy (%/hr) for 6 hours after vehicle or E2006 administration.
WT Mice: Lemborexant significantly increases NREM sleep time (10 mg/kg, 30 mg/kg) and REM sleep time (10 mg/kg, 30 mg/kg) compared to vehicle. Cataplexy time is significantly increased (10 mg/kg, 30 mg/kg).
Orexin KO Mice: Lemborexant significantly increases wake time (30 mg/kg) compared to vehicle. NREM sleep time is significantly increased (30 mg/kg). Cataplexy time is significantly increased (30 mg/kg).
L2286: Line graph showing the relationship between Lemborexant Dose (mg) on the X-axis (0 to 30 mg) and two outcomes on the Y-axis: Sleep Efficiency (Benefit) (% improvement relative to placebo) and Somnolence (Risk) (% patients). The Sleep Efficiency (Benefit) curve (blue line with triangles) increases steadily from 0% at 0 mg to approximately 9% at 25 mg. The Somnolence (Risk) curve (orange line with circles) increases sharply from 0% at 0 mg to approximately 22% at 25 mg.

L2289: Figure 5 displays the pharmacokinetic profiles of lemborexant in subjects with Normal, Mild, and Moderate Hepatic Impairment. The main graph plots Mean Lemborexant Plasma Concentration (ng/mL) on a logarithmic scale (0.1 to 100) against Time (h) (0 to 312). The inset graph plots Mean Lemborexant Plasma Concentration (ng/mL) on a linear scale (0 to 100) against Time (h) (0 to 12).
The legend indicates:
Normal Hepatic Function (N = 8) (Green circles)
Mild Hepatic Impairment (N = 8) (Blue squares)
Moderate Hepatic Impairment (N = 8) (Orange diamonds)
The main graph shows that subjects with Mild and Moderate Hepatic Impairment exhibit significantly higher mean plasma concentrations of lemborexant over the 312-hour period compared to subjects with Normal Hepatic Function. The inset graph shows the initial 12 hours, where all groups show similar peak concentrations around 2 hours, followed by a rapid decline.
L2290: Figure 6: Mean Plasma Concentration-Time Curve of Lemborexant Over 0 to 24 Hours by Treatment. The graph plots Concentration (ng/mL) on the Y-axis (0 to 80) against Nominal Time (h) on the X-axis (0 to 24). Two curves are shown: Fasted (blue line with open circles) and Fed (red line with solid circles). Both curves show a peak concentration around 2 to 3 hours. The Fasted group reaches a higher peak concentration (around 55 ng/mL) compared to the Fed group (around 35 ng/mL). The Fasted curve shows a faster decline after the peak. Error bars are included for both conditions.
L2291: Figure 7 is a forest plot illustrating the Mean Effect and 90% Confidence Interval (CI) of various interacting drugs on the pharmacokinetics of Lemborexant, comparing Cmax (maximum plasma concentration, represented by triangles) and AUC (area under the curve, represented by squares). The X-axis represents the Change Relative to Reference (without interacting drug), ranging from 0.0 to 5.0. A vertical shaded bar indicates the range of 1.0 to 1.5, representing the reference range.
The interacting drugs listed are:
Strong CYP3A Inducer: Rifampin (600 mg)
Strong CYP3A Inhibitor: Itraconazole (200 mg)
Moderate CYP3A Inhibitor: Fluconazole (200 mg)
Alcohol (Female: 0.6 g/kg, Male: 0.7 g/kg)
Gastric pH Modifier: Famotidine (40 mg)
Oral Contraceptive: Ethinyl Estradiol (0.030 mg) + Norethindrone (1.5 mg)
Note: For some parameters, the 90% CIs are narrow and not visible.
L2292: Figure 8 is a line graph showing Mean (±SE) Body Sway (1/3 Angle of Arc) on the Y-axis (ranging from 20 to 100) versus Scheduled Time Point (h) on the X-axis (ranging from Pre 0.5 to 72 hours). Four conditions are plotted:
Placebo (Blue line with circles)
Lemborexant 10 mg (Red line with crosses)
Alcohol (Green line with asterisks)
Lemborexant 10 mg with Alcohol (Yellow line with squares)
The graph illustrates that body sway generally peaks around 2 to 6 hours post-administration and then decreases, stabilizing around 40 to 50 degrees of arc by 24 hours. The Lemborexant 10 mg with Alcohol condition shows the highest peak body sway (around 90 degrees) at 2 hours, followed by the Placebo condition (around 85 degrees). The Placebo and Lemborexant 10 mg conditions show similar body sway profiles, while the Alcohol condition shows a lower peak body sway (around 65 degrees) at 2 hours.
L2293: Figure 9 displays two line graphs comparing cognitive performance metrics (Power of Attention and Continuity of Attention) across four treatment groups: Placebo, Lemborexant 10 mg, Alcohol, and Lemborexant 10 mg with Alcohol, measured at scheduled time points (Pre, 0.5, 2, 6, 9, 12, 24, 48, 72 hours).
Power of Attention
The graph shows Mean (±SE) Power of Attention (Y-axis, ranging from 1250 to 2250). All groups start near 2000. Lemborexant 10 mg with Alcohol shows a sharp decline starting around 2 hours, reaching approximately 1250 by 6 hours, and remaining low. Placebo, Lemborexant 10 mg, and Alcohol groups remain relatively stable, fluctuating between 1250 and 1300.
Continuity of Attention
The graph shows Mean (±SE) Continuity of Attention (Y-axis, ranging from 70 to 95). All groups start near 90. Lemborexant 10 mg with Alcohol shows a sharp decline starting around 2 hours, reaching approximately 75 by 6 hours, and remaining low. Placebo, Lemborexant 10 mg, and Alcohol groups remain relatively stable, fluctuating between 85 and 90.

L2301: Figure 16 is a timeline diagram illustrating the Study Design (E2006-G000-303). The study is divided into two main phases: Prerandomization and Randomization, spanning a total duration of 12 months plus 2 weeks.
Prerandomization Period:
Screening: Starts between Day -35 and Day -17.
Run-In: Starts between Day -17 and Day -1, ending on Day -1.
Baseline: Occurs on Day 1.
Randomization Period:
Subjects are randomized (R) on Day 1. The study then proceeds through two treatment periods and a follow-up phase.
Treatment Period 1 (6 months):
Subjects randomized to Lemborexant 5 mg or Lemborexant 10 mg receive the corresponding dose.
Treatment Period 2 (12 months):
Subjects randomized to Placebo receive Placebo.
Subjects randomized to Lemborexant 5 mg or Lemborexant 10 mg receive the corresponding dose.
Follow-Up (+2 weeks):
All subjects receive No Study Drug.
The diagram visually represents the staggered start of the Run-In period relative to the Screening period, and the staggered start of the Randomization period relative to the Baseline period.

L2311: Figure 26 displays three line graphs showing the persistence of effectiveness on subjective sleep onset latency (sSOL), subjective sleep efficiency (sSE), and wake after sleep onset (sWASO) over 12 months of Lemborexant treatment in Study 303. The graphs compare Lemborexant 5 mg (N=443) and Lemborexant 10 mg (N=437).
Top Graph: sSOL (min)
The Y-axis represents sSOL in minutes (0 to 60). Both doses show a rapid decrease in sSOL from Baseline (around 50 min) to 1 month (around 30 min). sSOL remains relatively stable and low (around 20-25 min) through 12 months, with the 5 mg dose slightly lower than the 10 mg dose at 12 months.
Middle Graph: sSE (%)
The Y-axis represents sSE in percent (0 to 110). Both doses show a slight increase in sSE from Baseline (around 65%) to 1 month (around 70%), followed by a slight decrease and stabilization around 75-80% through 12 months.
Bottom Graph: sWASO (min)
The Y-axis represents sWASO in minutes (0 to 170). Both doses show a rapid decrease in sWASO from Baseline (around 125 min) to 1 month (around 100 min). sWASO remains relatively stable and low (around 80-90 min) through 12 months, with the 5 mg dose slightly lower than the 10 mg dose at 12 months.
L2312: Figure 27: Study Design, E2006-G000-304
The study design is divided into Prerandomization and Randomization phases.
Prerandomization:
Screening: ≥7 days but not >14 days. No treatment.
Run-In: ≥7 days but not >17 days. Placebo at bedtime each night.
BL (D1): Baseline (Day 1).
Assessments during Prerandomization include In Clinic Screening, Screening PSGa, and BL PSGsa.
Randomization:
Treatment: ≥28 days but not >35 days. LEM5, LEM 10, ZOL, or PBO at bedtime each night.
Follow-Up: ≥14 days but not >18 days. No Treatment.
Assessments during Randomization include D1 & D2 PSGsa and D29 & D30 PSGsa.
L2313: Flow Chart of Gate-Keeping Testing Procedure
Legend:
Yes = statistically significant at 5% level
No = not statistically significant at 5% level
Stop = stop testing procedure
The flow chart is structured into three main sections: Primary Endpoints, Secondary Endpoints, and US Only / Non-US Only analyses.
Primary Endpoints:
LPS (LEM10 vs PBO): If Yes, proceed to LPS (LEM5 vs PBO). If No, Stop.
LPS (LEM5 vs PBO): If Yes, proceed to SE (LEM10 vs PBO). If No, Stop.
Secondary Endpoints:
SE (LEM10 vs PBO): If Yes, proceed to SE (LEM5 vs PBO). If No, Stop.
SE (LEM5 vs PBO): If Yes, proceed to US Only analysis. If No, Stop.
US Only Analysis:
WASO (LEM10 vs PBO): If Yes, proceed to WASO2H (LEM10 vs ZOL). If No, Stop.
WASO2H (LEM10 vs ZOL): If Yes, proceed to WASO (LEM5 vs PBO). If No, Stop.
WASO (LEM5 vs PBO): If Yes, proceed to WASO2H (LEM5 vs ZOL). If No, Stop.
WASO2H (LEM5 vs ZOL): If Yes, proceed to Non-US Only analysis. If No, Stop.
Non-US Only Analysis:
WASO (LEM10 vs PBO): If Yes, proceed to WASO (LEM5 vs PBO). If No, Stop.
WASO (LEM5 vs PBO): If Yes, proceed to Non-US Only analysis. If No, Stop.

The Placebo group shows the highest probability of not experiencing somnolence, starting around 0.99 and remaining stable. The ZOL 6.25 mg group starts slightly lower (around 0.98) and remains stable. The LEM 5 mg and LEM 10 mg groups show the lowest probabilities, starting around 0.97 and decreasing steadily over time, reaching approximately 0.91 by 60 days.

L2340: Figure 55: Dose Normalized Lemborexant Concentrations in Subject Taking PPI and Not Taking PPI (No PPI). The left plot, labeled 'No PPI', shows multiple data series (Studies 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 8, 106, 107, 108, 201, 304) exhibiting a rapid decline in concentration over 25 hours, with concentrations ranging from 0.01 to 10.00 ug/mL/mg. The right plot, labeled 'PPI', shows significantly lower concentrations, primarily clustered between 0.01 and 0.10 ug/mL/mg, indicating a substantial reduction in lemborexant exposure when taken with PPIs.

L2343: Figure 58 is a line graph titled "Effect of itraconazole on the hepatic intrinsic clearance of lemborexant". The Y-axis represents Intrinsic Clearance (L/h) ranging from 0 to 250. The X-axis represents time in [Hour] ranging from 0 to 336. Two simulated profiles are shown: "simulated using Simcyp ITZ model, fasted solution" (blue line) and "simulated using IQ ITZ model, fasted capsule" (orange line). Both profiles show a sharp increase in intrinsic clearance around 1 hour, stabilizing between approximately 50 and 150 L/h for the remainder of the 336-hour period. The orange line (IQ ITZ model, fasted capsule) consistently shows a higher intrinsic clearance than the blue line (Simcyp ITZ model, fasted solution).
